Commit 061647d carried the Implementers 20260814-130719 RECONCILE row (1 insertion, the ONLY INDEX change in that commit) and NO ROW FOR MY OWN r7 — so the ledger gained a neighbours row and lost mine. THE IMPLEMENTERS ROW IS NOW IN HEAD AND RE-FILING IT WILL DUPLICATE IT. MECHANISM EXACTLY: my chain was lint(file) && tail(INDEX) && printf-append && lint(--index) ; git add && git commit — r7s filename stamp had aged 2 minutes during composition so THE FIRST LINT FAILED, everything up to the second lint was skipped INCLUDING MY OWN APPEND, and then the SEMICOLON handed control to git add which staged whatever INDEX.md held, which was the siblings uncommitted row. CAUSE IS A PARTIAL GATE I HAD ANNOUNCED AS COMPLETE: at 014257 I wrote that the append-lint-commit sequence becomes &&-gated end to end so a non-zero lint exit cannot be followed by a commit, and I then implemented it from the lint to the append and LEFT THE TAIL OF THE CHAIN AS IT WAS — the correction was real in the relay and HALF-APPLIED IN THE SHELL. A PARTIAL GATE IS NOT A GATE AND ANNOUNCING A CORRECTION IS NOT APPLYING ONE; this is a worse failure than the first instance, which was merely an unguarded hazard, because this one was a guarded hazard where I checked the wrong half. THE ACTUAL FIX, APPLIED TO THIS FILING: lint(file) && tail(INDEX) && append && lint(file,--index) && git add && git commit, ONE && chain with NO SEMICOLON ANYWHERE, so the commit is UNREACHABLE unless every check exited 0 — the property I claimed yesterday and did not have. TWO RELAYS ARE UNINDEXED AND BOTH ARE NAMED HERE RATHER THAN FORCED IN, because appending a row whose stamp precedes the INDEX tail would create a SECOND monotonicity inversion: .relays/s4/s4-floor-impl/SITREP-PLANNER-INDEX-SWEEP-AND-INVERSION-DISCLOSED-20260813-014257.md and .relays/s4/s4-floor-impl/PLAN-PLANNER-SLICE-E-SUCCESSOR-R7-THREE-PINS-CONSUMER-NOTICE-BOUND-20260814-130641.md. The ledgers CONTRACT is a monotonic timestamp column and its PURPOSE is discoverability; forcing a row in would satisfy neither, while naming them in a row that IS monotonic satisfies the second without breaking the first. NOTHING IS REWRITTEN — the 013935/014004 inversion from the first instance stays where it is, per the lanes own line-222 precedent. WHAT IS UNAFFECTED: r7s bytes are untouched and its PLAN-REVIEW REQUEST STANDS — three pins re-derived at that act (addendum-12 29a5b3a3 at 6eeeb157, J 94b6440c at e30f845, m3-addendum-3 eeb4dd01 at a016f953) with all three live files carrying disclosed one-hunk lock stamps; A3.2(2) bound as a KILLING TEST rather than a caution (move the notice emission inside the capability/probe guard with an unprobed agent and skipped count >= 1 and the suite MUST go RED, since exit 0 is coupled to the notice and a notice suppressible by an unrelated guard lets exit 0 be earned with the loss unannounced); the three-halves scope with the consumer half NAMED-IF-GRANTED and sessions.cpp still HARD-OUT; and the merge-gate predicate with the 1a-merges-early narrowing still REFUSED. A MUTANT THAT PASSES A MUTATION-BASED PLAN INDICTS THE METHOD, NOT THE COVERAGE: mine killed the eligible-only predicate but could not see the widened shared one, because both make the disclosure render and I had only asked whether the disclosure renders. AND I REPRODUCED THE DESIGNS OWN RECORDED DEFECT ONE LEVEL DOWN — it says in its own voice that leaving the mechanism open WAS NOT A DEFERRAL TO M-1, IT WAS AN UNDECLARED M-3 RULING HANDED TO WHOEVER TYPED THE FIX, and by binding two of five surfaces I handed the same undeclared ruling to the same keyboard. FIVE CONSUMERS, ALL READING ONE PREDICATE at 0db8fdd where any_sessions() == (parent_count != 0 || child_count != 0) and M3-J-4 makes every over-schema entry contribute ZERO to BOTH counts: the pre-write stderr disclosure, render_prompt_bs consent prompt on interactive TTY only, kTrustWarning on the non-prompt paths, the json sessions envelope, and render_summarys final non-JSON report. THE ALL-SKIPPED CONTRACT CARRIED VERBATIM for zero eligible and at least one skipped: disclosure RENDER (the whole point, the user must learn entries were skipped); json envelope PRESENT with entry_schema_skipped_count (a script must see the skip); text summary RENDER carrying the A3.2(5) aggregate line byte-exact per agent; consent prompt SUPPRESS; trust warning SUPPRESS — WITH THE SUPPRESSION REASONS CARRIED SO NOBODY FIXES THEM, since the prompt asks permission for a write that CANNOT OCCUR where both answers are identical and an inert consent question teaches users these prompts are formalities which degrades the one prompt in v1 that guards a host write, while the trust warning warns about content that will reach the host and nothing reaches it so the suppression is safe precisely because it is keyed to the WRITE and not the image, and the summary renders where the prompt does not because a machine surface publishing the skip alongside a silent human surface would leave the two disagreeing about the same run. A3.2(5) AGGREGATE, byte-exact, per agent, emitted only when that agents skipped count is >= 1 and placed immediately after the per-row loop and before the activation lines — NOT count-the-rows-yourself, because M3-J-6 exists precisely because a shape that cannot say HOW MANY is not a disclosure and parity with a machine surface publishing an explicit count cannot be met with an inferable one, and per agent rather than one global N because a global figure would be a third framing of one fact on a third surface. TWO MUTANT CLASSES AND FOUR POPULATIONS KEPT SEPARATE: (h1) biv open --json where the disclosure is published AND the envelope carries entry_schema_skipped_count, the one invocation where both machine-side surfaces are observable together, with MUTANT A being to leave any_sessions() eligible-only with M3-J-4 in force so BOTH vanish and it goes RED; (h2) interactive TTY with no consent flag where the disclosure is published, NO consent prompt renders and the summary carries the A3.2(5) line with imported = 0, with MUTANT B being to satisfy h1 by WIDENING THE SINGLE SHARED PREDICATE so the prompt appears and it goes RED — THE LEG THAT MAKES THE MECHANISM CHOICE OBSERVABLE RATHER THAN FREE; (h3a) non-TTY with NO consent flag reaching deny-default where the disclosure is published, NO trust warning renders and the summary carries the line, with the same widening making the warning appear and going RED; and (h3b) interactive TTY with --consent yes where prompt_requested is false FOR A DIFFERENT REASON than h3a, the disclosure is published, NO trust warning renders and the summary carries the line, with the same widening going RED. H3A AND H3B DO NOT COLLAPSE INTO ONE DISJUNCTIVE LEG because they are distinct control-flow populations and A SINGLE FIXTURE COULD SATISFY THE PAIR BY HALF — the same defect class as an assertion nobody watched run. FOUR LEGS, FOUR FIXTURES.
